<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Pius III</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg/60px-C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="72" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg/90px-C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg/120px-C_o_a_Piccolomini_Popes.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1031" data-file-height="1238" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data">None</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Pius III</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Pio III</i>; 9 May 1439&#160;– 18 October 1503),<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> born <b>Francesco Todeschini</b>, was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> from 22 September 1503 to his death. At just twenty-six days, he had one of the shortest pontificates in papal history.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francesco was the nephew of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_II" title="Pope Pius II">Pope Pius II</a>, who granted him the use of the family name "Piccolomini", and appointed the twenty-one-year old Francesco as <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Siena" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Siena">Archbishop of Siena</a>. He served as papal legate in a number of places. In 1503, the now-frail Francesco, known as Cardinal Piccolomini, was elected pope as a compromise candidate between the Borgia and della Rovere factions. Although he announced plans for reforms, he died less than a month later.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Life">Life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Francesco Todeschini Piccolomini, a member of the <a href="/wiki/Piccolomini" title="Piccolomini">House of Piccolomini</a> was born in <a href="/wiki/Sarteano" title="Sarteano">Sarteano</a><sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> on 9 May 1439,<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> as the fourth child of Nanno Todeschini<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> and Laudomia Piccolomini, the sister of Enea Silvio Bartolomeo (Aeneas Silvius) Piccolomini who was <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_II" title="Pope Pius II">Pope Pius II</a>. He had three brothers, Antonio, Giacomo and Andrea. His eldest brother Antonio was made Duke of <a href="/wiki/Amalfi" title="Amalfi">Amalfi</a> during the pontificate of Pius II. He married Maria, the daughter of King <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_I_of_Naples" title="Ferdinand I of Naples">Ferdinand I of Naples</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francesco was received as a boy into the household of Aeneas Silvius who permitted him to assume the name and arms of the Piccolomini family. He studied Canon Law at the university of <a href="/wiki/Perugia" title="Perugia">Perugia</a>, and obtained a <a href="/wiki/Doctorate" title="Doctorate">doctorate</a> after the completion of his studies.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinalate">Cardinalate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Cardinalate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1457,<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Todeschini-Piccolomini was granted the office of Provost of the <a href="/wiki/Collegiate_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Collegiate Church">Collegiate Church</a> of Sankt Viktor in Xanten (later <a href="/wiki/Xanten_Cathedral" title="Xanten Cathedral">Xanten Cathedral</a>), which had been a benefice of his uncle.  Francesco held the benefice from 1457 to 1466, and again from 1476 to 1495.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal Aeneas Silvius Piccolomini was elected pope as <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_II" title="Pope Pius II">Pius II</a> on 19 August 1458. In the excited tumult following the announcement, the Roman mob sacked his house, which was located near the church of S. Agostino, not far from the north end of the Piazza Navona; even the marble stones were taken. When the Piccolomini family arrived in Rome, therefore, they had no palazzo of their own to use as their base of operations. Francesco moved into the Vatican Palace with his uncle.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Pius II was aware that this was a temporary situation; he remarked in a letter to his nephew Antonio that "One is not the nephew of a pope forever (<i>non-semper pontificis nepos</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> In 1461, the Pope authorized Cardinal Francesco to purchase a property near the Campo dei Fiori in Rome which had belonged to the recently deceased Cardinal Giovanni Castiglione. The documents made it clear that it was not the Pope or the Papacy which were buying the property, but the Piccolomini family, and that it was private property, not property of the Church, even though Cardinal Francesco's deaconry was not far distant. On this land, Cardinal Francesco, with the Pope's help, built the Piccolomini Palace. In 1476, Cardinal Francesco deeded the palace to his brothers Giacomo and Andrea, and their descendants, on the condition that it not be alienated from the male line. The Palazzo Piccolomini no longer survives, having been razed to make room for the new church of S. Andrea della Valle, which was begun in 1591.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Piccolomini already held the office of protonotary apostolic<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> at the time that he was appointed the administrator of the <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Siena" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Siena">Archdiocese of Siena</a><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> in 1460.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He was granted the title and the insignia of an archbishop, but he did not receive <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_consecration" class="mw-redirect" title="Episcopal consecration">episcopal consecration</a> until a week before his coronation as pope. The episcopal duties at Siena were carried out by an auxiliary bishop, <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Fatati" title="Antonio Fatati">Antonio Fatati</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Pius II, who was visiting Siena at the time, appointed his nephew a cardinal on 5 March 1460, naming him <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Eustachio" title="Sant&#39;Eustachio">Cardinal-Deacon of Sant'Eustachio</a> on 26 March.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was also named <a href="/wiki/Commendatory_abbot" title="Commendatory abbot">commendatory abbot</a> of the monastery of S. Vigilio in Siena.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He reconstructed and extended the residence next to the church, which he continued to use throughout his life.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1460, the Pope appointed him legate of the <a href="/wiki/Ancona" title="Ancona">March of Ancona</a>, with the experienced <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Marsico" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Marsico">Bishop of Marsico</a> as his counsellor. He departed Rome on 30 April, and returned on 1 February 1461 for consultations; he returned to Ancona on 1 June 1461, and was back in Rome on 8 November.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He proved studious and effective in his job.
</p><p>Piccolomini was made the archdeacon of Brabant in <a href="/wiki/Cambrai" title="Cambrai">Cambrai</a> in 1462 and he held that benefice until 1503.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> On 26 March 1463, Pope Pius II granted Cardinal Francesco the monastery of <a href="/wiki/San_Saba,_Rome" title="San Saba, Rome">San Saba</a> on the Aventine Hill <i>in commendam</i>. The cardinal immediately began extensive restoration, construction, and decoration works on the ancient buildings, spending at least 3,000 ducats on the work.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Piccolomini was named Vicar of <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> and the rest of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> on 21 June 1464, as Pius II departed Rome for <a href="/wiki/Ancona" title="Ancona">Ancona</a>, where he intended to meet the Venetians and launch a crusade in the Balkans. However, Pius II died at Ancona on 14 August 1464, terminating the project.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Conclaves_of_1464_and_1471">Conclaves of 1464 and 1471</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Conclaves of 1464 and 1471">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Francesco Todeschini Piccolomini participated in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave" title="Papal conclave">conclave</a> that elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_II" title="Pope Paul II">Pope Paul II</a> in 1464. As a nephew of the late pope, he should have had considerable influence in the politics of the election. Of the twenty cardinals who participated, however, the twelve who had not been named by Pius II agreed among themselves that they would not vote to elect anyone except one of themselves. This excluded Francesco Piccolomini and all of his uncle's cardinals. As it happened, the first vote was still in progress when Cardinal Pietro Barbo of Venice received the required two-thirds of the votes, and the scrutiny was quickly made unanimous. He chose the name Paul II (1464–1471).<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal Piccolomini was named <i>Legatus de latere</i> in Germany on 20 February 1471.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He was accompanied as his secretary by Agostino Patrizi Piccolomini, the former private secretary of Pius II, who wrote an account of the mission.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> He departed on 18 March,<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and served in this important legation for the <a href="/wiki/Diet_of_Ratisbon_(1541)" class="mw-redirect" title="Diet of Ratisbon (1541)">Imperial diet at Regensburg/Ratisbon</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> and was still there when the Pope died on 26 July 1471. Consequently, he was absent for the Conclave of 1471 which elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_IV" title="Pope Sixtus IV">Pope Sixtus IV</a>. He returned to Rome on 27 December 1471.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He succeeded to the position of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Protodeacon" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Protodeacon">Cardinal Protodeacon</a> in 1471, upon the promotion of Cardinal Rodrigo Borgia to the see of Albano on 30 August 1471.
</p><p>Francesco served in a new legation for <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_IV" title="Pope Sixtus IV">Pope Sixtus IV</a>, to restore ecclesiastical authority in <a href="/wiki/Umbria" title="Umbria">Umbria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Conclaves_of_1484_and_1492">Conclaves of 1484 and 1492</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Conclaves of 1484 and 1492">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Todeschini-Piccolomini participated in the conclave of 1484 which resulted in the election of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_VIII" title="Pope Innocent VIII">Pope Innocent VIII</a>, and as the protodeacon he made the first public announcement of the election and <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">crowned</a> the new pope. According to Stefano Infessura, he was one of the half-dozen cardinals who had slept soundly in their beds on the night between 28 August and 29 August, and had not participated in the clandestine midnight conferences that produced a two-thirds majority for Cardinal Giovanni Battista Cibo. Neither had he engaged in the extensive simoniac trading that took place.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was made the administrator of <a href="/wiki/Fermo" title="Fermo">Fermo</a> in 1485; he resigned the position in 1494, in favor of Agostino Piccolomini. He was reappointed when Agostino resigned in 1496, and he kept that post until his election to the Papacy.
</p><p>He was appointed papal legate to Perugia on 5 November 1488, and departed Rome on 15 November. He served in Perugia until 1489.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Todeschini-Piccolomini participated in the conclave of 1492 which elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_VI" title="Pope Alexander VI">Pope Alexander VI</a>. He belonged to the faction of the more senior cardinals who gathered around Cardinal Oliviero Carafa of Naples. Cardinal Francesco was sufficiently respected that he received six votes at the first scrutiny (Sixteen were needed to elect), seven on the second, and one on the third. He resisted the election of Cardinal Rodrigo Borgia almost to the end, as one of the five hold-outs.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> As Cardinal Protodeacon Piccolomini announced and crowned the new pontiff.
</p><p>He served as the protector of <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a> at the Roman Curia from 1492 to 1503,<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> and of <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a>.
</p><p>He was appointed legate to King <a href="/wiki/Charles_VIII_of_France" title="Charles VIII of France">Charles VIII of France</a>, whose army was then entering Tuscany, in the consistory of 1 October 1494, departing Rome on 17 October; he returned to Rome on 5 March 1495, after the King declined to meet him.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> On 27 May 1495, he and numerous other cardinals accompanied <a href="/wiki/Pope_Alexander_VI" title="Pope Alexander VI">Pope Alexander VI</a> on a visit to Orvieto, which had been arranged to avoid a meeting between the Pope and King Charles, who was returning from his expedition against Naples. Charles was in Rome from 1 to 4 June, and the Pope and his retinue returned to the city on 27 June.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was named the administrator of the diocese of <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Pienza#Diocese_of_Pienza_e_Montalcino" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Pienza">Pienza and Montalcino</a> on 31 October 1495, and occupied it until 14 March 1498, when he resigned in favor of his relative, Girolamo Piccolomini.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the murder of his son <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Borgia_(1474%E2%80%931497)" class="mw-redirect" title="Giovanni Borgia (1474–1497)">Giovanni Borgia</a> in 1497, Alexander VI appointed Francesco Piccolomini a member of a commission of six cardinals, in a short-lived effort to reform the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> On 8 February 1501, Pope Alexander also appointed Piccolomini, in his capacity as Protodeacon, to a commission to take charge of the income from the tithe (<i>decuma</i>), and dispensing it for yet another contemplated crusade against the Turks.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Piccolomini_Library">The Piccolomini Library</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: The Piccolomini Library">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1502 he commissioned a library with access from an aisle of the <a href="/wiki/Duomo_di_Siena" class="mw-redirect" title="Duomo di Siena">Duomo di Siena</a> that was intended to house the library of <a href="/wiki/Renaissance_humanism" title="Renaissance humanism">humanist texts</a> assembled by his uncle. Francesco commissioned the artist <a href="/wiki/Pinturicchio" title="Pinturicchio">Pinturicchio</a> to fresco its vault and ten narrative panels along the walls, depicting scenes from the life of <a href="/wiki/Aeneas_Silvius_Piccolomini" class="mw-redirect" title="Aeneas Silvius Piccolomini">Aeneas Silvius Piccolomini</a>. Its iconography illustrating the donor's career gives an edited version of Pius II's life, passing over his former support of the <a href="/wiki/Antipope" title="Antipope">Antipope</a> <a href="/wiki/Felix_V" class="mw-redirect" title="Felix V">Felix V</a>. Though Pinturicchio labored for five years, the books never reached their splendid destination;<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag needs further explanation. (February 2020)">further explanation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> yet the Piccolomini Library is a monument of the <a href="/wiki/Renaissance_art" title="Renaissance art">High Renaissance</a> in Siena.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Some of Pope Pius III's most famous portraits can be viewed in the Louvre Museum.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Pontificate">Pontificate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Pontificate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_to_papacy">Election to papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Election to papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_September_1503" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, September 1503">Papal conclave, September 1503</a></div>
<p>Pope Alexander VI died on 18 August 1503,<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> and amid the disturbances consequent upon his death, it took the combined pressures of all the ambassadors in Rome to induce <a href="/wiki/Caesar_Borgia" class="mw-redirect" title="Caesar Borgia">Cesare Borgia</a> to withdraw from the city, so that an unpressured conclave might take place. Despite urgent pleas of the cardinals to stay away, both the Orsini and the Colonna factions entered the city with troops, intending to avenge old and new grievances.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Because of these negotiations, the Conclave did not begin until 16 September.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal Piccolomini was elected on 22 September 1503 and took the name "Pius III" after his uncle Pius II.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> This selection can be seen as a compromise between factions, <a href="/wiki/Borgia" class="mw-redirect" title="Borgia">Borgia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Della_Rovere" title="Della Rovere">della Rovere</a>, picking a frail cardinal with long experience in the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a> over the kin of either Sixtus IV or Alexander VI.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Programme">Programme</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Programme">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 25 September the new pontiff held an unusual Consistory meeting of cardinals and other officials, including the ambassadors of several states. Normally, a pope did not hold such meetings until after his coronation, but Pius III was faced with an emergency, and he was being hard pressed by the Spanish cardinals.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> A French army, which was nominally under the command of Cesare Borgia, who was ill and in bed, was demanding passage through Rome in order to attack the Spanish government in Naples. Naples was a papal fief, which complicated diplomacy. At the consistory, Pius first announced his desire to bring about peace between the kings of France and Spain. Then he promulgated the aims of his pontificate:<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> the immediate reform of the church, with the establishment of a council of cardinals; strict reform of the expenses and financial situation of the church; peace in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>; and the support of Cesare Borgia, now without his French support, against his enemies who were planning to murder him. The next day, he told the Venetian ambassador, Antonio Giustinian: "In consequence of the pressure put upon me by the Spanish cardinals, I have been compelled to some briefs in favour of Cesare Borgia, but I will not give him any further help. I do not intend to be a warlike, but a peace-loving pope."<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 September, Pius III granted permission for 8,500 French soldiers to pass by Rome, but not across the Milvian Bridge (Ponte Molle)
</p><p>Pius supported <a href="/wiki/Cesare_Borgia" title="Cesare Borgia">Cesare Borgia</a>, and reconfirmed him as <a href="/wiki/Gonfalonier_of_the_Church" title="Gonfalonier of the Church">Gonfalonier</a>. He allowed him to come into the city of Rome from his refuge at Nepi, aware that Bartolomeo d'Alviano was hurrying with forces from Venice to murder Borgia.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Illness_and_death">Illness and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_III&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Illness and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On the morning of 26 September, the newly elected pope underwent an operation on his ulcerous left leg, enduring the pain of cutting in two places.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> The next day, he announced that he would not carry out the ceremony of the possession of his cathedral on the day of the coronation, as the custom was, because of his lameness.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Piccolomini was never ordained a priest, remaining in diaconal orders, until 30 September 1503, when he finally received <a href="/wiki/Ordination" title="Ordination">ordination</a>. Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Pope_Julius_II" title="Pope Julius II">Giuliano della Rovere</a> ordained him in one of the halls of the papal palace, with the Pope seated the whole time. Della Rovere consecrated him a bishop on Sunday, 1 October 1503, in the same hall in the Vatican, assisted by the Bishop of Savona (Aldello de Piccolomini) and the Bishop of Spoleto (Francesco Eruli).<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A Venetian agent in Rome reported on 3 October that the Pope was suffering from a high fever and had severe pain in his leg. Some judged that he had little time to live, and the politicking for the next conclave was already beginning.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The coronation took place on 8 October 1503. Cardinal Raffaello Sansoni Riario, the Protodeacon, performed the coronation. Several of the features of the ritual had to be omitted due to Pius' troublesome leg. Ludwig Pastor notes that the Pope said Mass sitting.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On Thursday the 12th, as Beltrando Costabili reported to the Duke of Ferrara, Pope Pius had a long audience and did not eat during the day, having been taking medicine the previous day, on which the fever struck and never left him.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg/220px-Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg/330px-Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg/440px-Tomb_of_pope_Pius_III.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a><figcaption>Former tomb of Pius III</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 13 October he was on his deathbed, and after a brief pontificate of 26 days he died on 18 October 1503, of a septic <a href="/wiki/Ulcer_(dermatology)" title="Ulcer (dermatology)">ulcer</a> in his leg.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Some have alleged that Pope Pius died of <a href="/wiki/Poison" title="Poison">poison</a> administered at the instigation of <a href="/wiki/Pandolfo_Petrucci" title="Pandolfo Petrucci">Pandolfo Petrucci</a>, the ruler of Siena.<sup id="cite_ref-Novaes1804_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Novaes1804-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was buried in the chapel of San Andrea in Saint Peter's Basilica, next to his uncle Pius II, his brothers Giacomo and Andrea serving as his executors. He had already chosen his burial place when he wrote his Will of 1493.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> When the basilica was being rebuilt, the monument was transferred below to the grottoes and the remains of Pius III and his uncle to the church of San Andrea della Valle in Rome put in a mausoleum created by Cardinal Alessandro Damasceni Peretti in 1614.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>

<div style="clear:both;"></div>

<li id="cite_note-21"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-21">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">His appointment was confirmed on 23 April 1463, and he was obligated for annates from 9 January 1462; see: <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFHenry_Dubrulle1905" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source">Henry Dubrulle, ed. <li id="cite_note-51"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-51">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFMarino_Sanudo1881" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-long-vol cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source">Marino Sanudo (1881). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=Im1KAAAAYAAJ"><i>I diarii di Marino Sanuto: (MCCCCXCVI-MDXXXIII)</i></a> (in Italian). <li id="cite_note-Novaes1804-55"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Novaes1804_55-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The poisoning is mentioned by Onuphrio Panvinio as a suspicion, <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFBartolomeo_PlatinaOnuphrio_Panvinio1568" class="citation book cs1">Bartolomeo Platina; Onuphrio Panvinio (1568). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=_eFaAAAAQAAJ&amp;pg=PA364"><i>Historia B. Platinae de vitis pontificum Romanorum</i></a>. p.&#160;364.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Historia+B.+Platinae+de+vitis+pontificum+Romanorum&amp;rft.pages=364&amp;rft.date=1568&amp;rft.au=Bartolomeo+Platina&amp;rft.au=Onuphrio+Panvinio&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3D_eFaAAAAQAAJ%26pg%3DPA364&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+III" class="Z3988"></span>: "Pius interim senectae vitiis et mortifero cruris ulcere vehementer affectus, intra sextum et vigesimum pontificatus diem, XV Kalo. Novembris, non-sine veneni suspicione Pandulfi Petrucii Senensis tyranni consilio vulneri illiti in palatio Vaticano magno bonorum omnium dolore interiit, anno salutis MDIII, aedtatis lxiiij, mense quinto, die decimo...." The 16th century Sienese historian Malavolti is more discreet, but still labels the story an opinion: "Fù oppenione di piu persone, che per gelosia havuta da chi governava la Città di Siena, essendo egli di fattion contraria, la morte gli fusse, con danno publoicio, sollecitamente procurata." <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFOrlando_(di_M._Bernardo)_Malavolti1599" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-long-vol">Orlando (di M. Bernardo) Malavolti (1599). "Libro settimo". <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=x3hgAAAAcAAJ"><i>Dell'Historia Di Siena</i></a>. Vol.&#160;Terza parte. Siena: Per Salvestro Marchetti. pp.&#160;112, verso.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=bookitem&amp;rft.atitle=Libro+settimo&amp;rft.btitle=Dell%27Historia+Di+Siena&amp;rft.place=Siena&amp;rft.pages=112%2C+verso&amp;rft.pub=Per+Salvestro+Marchetti&amp;rft.date=1599&amp;rft.au=Orlando+%28di+M.+Bernardo%29+Malavolti&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3Dx3hgAAAAcAAJ&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+III" class="Z3988"></span> Also, <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFGiuseppe_de_Novaes1804" class="citation book cs1 cs1-prop-foreign-lang-source">Giuseppe de Novaes (1804). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=E3IXAAAAYAAJ&amp;pg=PA130"><i>Elementi della storia de' sommi pontefici da S. Pietro sino al felicemente regnante Pio Papa VII ...</i></a> (in Italian). Vol.&#160;VI (seconda&#160;ed.). Siena: Rossi. pp.&#160;130, note (a).</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Elementi+della+storia+de%27+sommi+pontefici+da+S.+Pietro+sino+al+felicemente+regnante+Pio+Papa+VII+...&amp;rft.place=Siena&amp;rft.pages=130%2C+note+%28a%29&amp;rft.edition=seconda&amp;rft.pub=Rossi&amp;rft.date=1804&amp;rft.au=Giuseppe+de+Novaes&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DE3IXAAAAYAAJ%26pg%3DPA130&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+III" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-56"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-56">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Richardson (1998), p. 201: "praesentium extendimus. Et insuper ob devotionem memoriam fel. nec. Pii PP II praedecessoris nostri et tui secundum carnem capella S. Andreae in basilica Principis Apostolorum sita quam idem praedecessor admodum et sumptuoso opere ornari procuravit sepulcrum nobilissimum tuo aere construi fecisti ut in dicta capella sepulturam eligere et ad ipsius sepulcri pedes libere et licite valeas tibi eisdem auctoritate et tenore de speciali gratia concedimus et indulgemus."</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-57"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-57">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFSalvador_Miranda" class="citation web cs1">Salvador Miranda. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://webdept.fiu.edu/~mirandas/bios1460.htm#Todeschini">"Todeschini-Piccolomini, Francesco (1439-1503)"</a>. Cardinals of the Holy Roman Church<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">21 February</span> 2015</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Todeschini-Piccolomini%2C+Francesco+%281439-1503%29&amp;rft.pub=Cardinals+of+the+Holy+Roman+Church&amp;rft.au=Salvador+Miranda&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwebdept.fiu.edu%2F~mirandas%2Fbios1460.htm%23Todeschini&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+Pius+III" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
